"FIVE XEATS wiTHour TRIAL, 


One of the most flagrant abuses

penses: : 


UNDEREED "PRISONERS, 


Another’ evil ‘that has been dis- 
closed and for which remedies’ are 
suggested is the practise on the part 
of gaolers-of taking squeeze. out, of |: 
| the allowance far food for the pri- 
soners, 

This allowance is ‘mich too low 
at-the present time, it is declared. 
Only ten cents a day is allowed per 
prisoner... But in spite. of ‘this, 
there. are’ ‘cases. reported in which 


_| gaolers have taken..a considerable 


squeeze out of the food allowance: 
with the result that there are many 
underfed men in the, prisons: 


To correct this condition, the De- 
partment. proposes that the food 
allowance be raised immediately to 
fifteen or twenty cents. ‘per day per. 
prisoner, and that special officers be 
assigned to- make regular inspee- 
tion of prisoners’ food and to ascer- 
tain that there. is no squeeze taken 
‘by gaclers. Incase tho full amount 
is not being spent - for food, the 
gaolers responsible are to bee severe~ 
ly panned:

ONE SECRET 


‘Charm, we know, is indefinable. 
Or at least we should know. it.if 
we are in the, habit of believing: 
that which we are often told. But 
it may be-that.lazy people -have 
given’ charm its name of. indefin- 
ableness.. They‘ cannot bother to

The two few British battleships 
Rodney and Nelson which are to 
enter service this year willbe ex- 
traordinary-looking raft. . 

In the new issue - of “Jane's 


Fighting Ships” the first’ authen-|5 
tic photographs as yet released by 


the Admiralty are reproduced. 


They show that the heavy guns |" 


will be carried in ‘three triple tur- 
rets (eneh mounting three 1Gin. 
guns), forward. A great armout- 
ed strueture, for all the world like 
the keep of a mediwyal castle, 


rises amidships, and has replaced | 


the qld-fashioned conning tower. 
< When completed they will.be un- 
usually: ugly vessels. Dr. Parkes 
is further able in this exceptional. 
ly fine issue of “Fighting Ships” 

to give a photographi and drawings 
of the new British armoured crui- 
gers of 10,000 tons. ‘They ure.dis- 
tinctly: disappointing. in compari- 
son with new United States-ships 
of the same size, of which also for 
“first time. particulars are 
given, oy. 

The ‘British “ships , cearry ‘eight 
Sin. and four 4in. guns; the Ameri- 
can ten Sin. and six bin. The Bri- 
tish boilers and machinery are ap- 
parently of normal type, whereas 
in the American ‘vessels extra high 
pressures. are adopted. 

One’ remarkable change in, the 
appearanes of the older. “United 
States’ battleships is’ recorded. 
The basketmast is being adandon- 


‘Jed and replaced by. ‘tripod - masts 


which-aore virtually of the British 
pattern. The, change has already 


; bee carried out in the Texas. 


A direction in which'the Ameri- 


can Navy hos .gone fast ‘ahend, 


is-in the development of aircraft 
fittings. > Thus .the new cruisers 
carry each, two catapults for Jaun- 
ching seaplunes; of which two are 
provided. They can be released 
by thig, means at.’ speed of a0 
miles an hour. . —

ever indispensable for study ‘of all 
naval, questions, and its photo- 
graphs and drawings are compl ate 
ands ‘technically ‘perfect. %

" CHIMES OF | ‘ST. PAUL'S : 
‘ SENT. Wa Ake 
= London, ‘Jan. 7. 
The London-New York wireless 
telephone service’ was formally 
inaugurated to-day. fi 


The first message to be hoard in 
‘New York, were:the chimes of St, 
Paul's, striking a-quarter-to-two, 
-sent ‘through a special telephone 
‘fixed in the south .tower of St. 
Pauls. After: this, Sir George 
Murray. (Secretary of the.Gené?al, 
Post Office). conversed with_ 


letters” and sometimes they are 
completely cut off from . thelr. 
‘families and friends. __ . 

To correct these abuses, it ‘is. 
proposed that thera be laws passed 
prohibiting interference | with 
prisoners’ ” mail. 
conditions should, be.immediatel, 
‘estabushed, Winter and summe:. 
‘clothes should be provided for the 
prisoners.’ There. should be a 
doctor in attendance. Sanitary 
conditions should be inspected and 
improved.” The grouhd-and floors 


-|of the gaols should be covered 


with a two-inch layer of cement 


and sand:to relieve, the dampness | 


Beds should be provided with 
varnished wood boards. to Prevent 
dampness and vermin. Mor: 
windows should be put in the’ 
gaols’ and bathrooms and ‘toilets 
be kept clean. 


VOCATIONAL TRAINING. 
In addition to these reforms, it 


is ‘proposed that vocational |train- | 


ing classes be ‘founded 
prigons, . 


in the’ 
in order ‘to. keep . the. 


| prisoners occupied while in prison 


and also-to equip them for a trade
